## Content re-tune: the all-ages capsule table and collection milestones (economy and collection lead, 2026-10-04)

With all eight content waves released the capsule pool is 208 cards (Common 88, Rare 64, Epic 40, Legendary 16; 88 before), and the A6.9 time-to-max targets failed by 2-3x. This lands the CONTENT_PLAN 8 re-tune (the request moved to `docs/requests/done/content-economy-retune.md`).
- **The all-ages table** (`capsules.allAges`, `src/content/capsules.ts`; DESIGN A6.4). From Arena 3 (Kingsmoat), where all eight ages and 176-208 of the 208 cards drop, every tier holds one more stack and more copies and Amber: Clay 3 stacks 6/2/2/2, 181 Amber; Bronze 4, 7/5/5/2, 363; Silver 5, 13/12/5/2, 916; Jade 6, 32/23/12/4, 2,419; Gold 7, 34/23/12/4, 4,562; Platinum 8, 34/28/12/4, 4,838; Aeon 9, 53/32/14/4, 6,221 (38.5 copies and 710.7 Amber per bag capsule before pity). The Age Capsule has 5 stacks there. Guarantees, odds, pity, Dust, skins, collection items and the bag are unchanged.
- **Why gated at Arena 3:** the plan's arena scaling. Arena 1 (44-card pool) and Arena 2 (102) are the first days of a save, and the onboarding script runs there: keeping their table exactly avoids an early Amber and copy flood on a small pool and leaves the A6.4 table, the scripted capsules and their tests byte-for-byte. One switch at Arena 3 (instead of a per-arena, per-rarity ratio) is simple to explain on the odds panel. Measured both ways: the flat option B reads 100.75 / 91.5 / 60 / 102.5 with a 31.5-day copy-Amber gap (10 seeds); the gated table 100 / 98 / 65 / 105 and a 23-day gap (30 seeds).
- **Tuning on the real pool** (the plan measured a synthetic clone pool): option B gated read Common 100 (6.5% faster than the pre-wave 107), so Common copies went ×0.95 and Amber ×0.96 (it also narrows the gap). Final, `economy --seeds 30`: Common / Rare / Epic / Legendary to max **105 / 97 / 62.25 / 106.5** days (before the re-tune: not reached / not reached / 187.5 / 222.75; a 730-day run gives 245.5 / 289 for Common and Rare), all 16 Legendaries owned day 24 (25), focused War Plan at L7 **94** (199.5), copies done 224.5 (not reached; 490.5 in 730 days), Amber done 210 (348), gap 15, whole collection **224.5 days** (7.4 months; target 7-7.5). Copies a day 240 (100), Amber 5,156 (3,279). Casual player (730 days): 196 / 165.25 / 114.25 / 186.25, collection 418.5 (before: Common and Rare not reached, 300.5 / 386.75). `drops --mode smoke` 17 of 17 before and after (stack counts now checked against the table of the arena the openings play in).
- **One code path:** `capsuleTierFor(capsules, tier, arenaIndex)` and `ageCapsuleStacksFor` in `src/content/capsuleTiers.ts` serve the roll (`meta/capsules/grant.ts`; the script always uses the base table), `meta/economy.ts` expected values (`arenaIndex` argument), the odds panel (`oddsModel` reads `save.arenaIndex`; a line says "Every age drops in your arena, so each capsule holds one more stack…" or "From Arena 3, where every age drops, …") and the tools (`drops`, the capsule bench's new "All-ages table" cases). Schema checks: no all-ages value below the base tier, room for every guarantee, every column rising up the ladder.
- **Targets (A6.9, `tools/economy.ts`):** time-to-max targets stay (110 / 101 / 69 / 112). Income rows rebased on the all-ages table, where the player spends nearly the whole year: 38 copies and 710 Amber per bag capsule, ~240 copies and ~5,150 Amber a day; all Legendaries ~3 weeks (16 now), whole collection ~7.25 months (copies 7.25, Amber 7). Every row passes except the focused War Plan at L7 (94 days against 42), an open Phase 3 item since before the waves (82.5 then); the model never crafts with Dust. The tool now also reports Dust a day and the collection milestone days (info rows), and the "All Legendaries" row no longer says 8.
- **Dust and pity unchanged, on purpose.** Dust income rises with copies (214 → 498 a day in days 11-120; 1.41 million in a year against 0.32, mostly copies past L10 once cards max), but the sinks grew in step (36 skins, about 48,000 Dust to craft, against 12 for about 16,000; 173 collection items) and per-card copy income (what crafting competes with) is unchanged, so prices stay. The Epic pity, the Legendary pity (all 16 Legendaries on day 24) and new-card protection (every capsule card on day 24) already fit the bigger pool.
- **Starter set:** a new save still owns exactly the 48 starter cards (each age's three original Common troops, two original turrets and its Anti-heavy Rare) and a 4-troop Stone plan; no wave card is a starter, and wave cards join the pools from Arena 2 (Commons) to 5 (Legendaries).
- **Collection milestones** (titles; DESIGN A5.8): Card Scout (own 100 troop and turret cards), Archivist (own every one), Master Smith (50 at L10), Grand Curator (every one at L10). Earned only by opening capsules and upgrading; an earned title is never taken away when a later release adds cards. New `TitleUnlock` kinds `cardsOwned`, `albumComplete`, `cardsMaxed`, `collectionMaxed`; `upgradeCard` now checks titles when a card reaches L10. Profile lists the four with progress bars ("62/208") under "Collection milestones" (phone and desktop screenshots in the session scratchpad `content/collection/`). Model days: 3, 24, 133 and 224.5, a ladder from the first days to month 7.5. A first draft "Age Master" (every card of one age at L10) measured day 202, too close to Grand Curator to be a separate goal, so it became the 50-card Master Smith.
- **UI scale check** (844x390 and 1280x720): the Card Album (272 entries, 34 per age, age chips scroll, filters sheet), the Army builder (Locked collapses to a row of silhouettes with "You own 15 of 34" and an Album link), the odds panel with the all-ages note, and capsule reveals of 3, 6 and 10 items (all-ages Clay, Jade and Aeon with a skin; the summary grid wraps and scrolls on phones) all read fine; no layout change was needed.

## Release check: the eight content waves and the capsule re-tune (release checker, 2026-10-07)

Gates, sessions and the whole tree after all eight waves and the economy re-tune. Every gate ran on this tree and, for comparison, on the published `6d399b1` (`main`, before the waves) with the same seeds.

- **Regression fixed: Medieval and Modern cards had drifted outside ±5** (per-card sweep, n = 400, tier V Balanced, L7, one-age window). Kennel Master +23.0, Brigand +14.1, Squires +11.5 (the Medieval wave reported +1.0 / +0.9 / +0.9), Commando -6.0 (+1.6), Rifle Grenadier -6.4 (-5.0). The Medieval readings are the same at `8f284e9`, the first commit with the wave, and with that commit's counter matrix, so the wave's last sweep ran on a tree state that was never committed; the Modern ones moved with later counter regenerations. New numbers (counters regenerated, input hash `7ab8ff00`; content hash `cba469d0`): Kennel Master a hound every 9 s, at most 2 (what its card text already said; was 6.5 s, 3) +1.6; Brigand damage 26 (27) -1.1; Squires 2 × 131 HP, 15 (16) -4.5; Commando 380 HP, 48 (368, 47) -1.3; Rifle Grenadier 210 HP, 42 (205, 39) -4.1. Two-age windows read higher, as for the earlier Raiders and squads (Kennel Master +5.5, Squires +6.3, Brigand +11.9, Commando +6.9, Rifle Grenadier +4.1). Each regeneration moves single cards by several points (Squires at 124 HP read -1.6 before and -9.4 after one), so the final values were measured after the last regeneration. The other waves' cards read exactly as reported (Gunpowder, Industrial, Future) or within 2 points (Stone, Bronze, Cosmic, the rest of Modern); the other Medieval cards within 3.
- **Per-card sweep, all 200 non-baseline cards in their one-age window:** the 136 wave cards: 117 → 123 within ±5. The 13 left are the ones the waves reported: five lane volleys held to the lane caps (Pebble Hail -30.6, Longbow Volley -17.1, Shrapnel Shells -25.3, Creeping Barrage -23.9, Meteor Drizzle -26.4), Sandstorm -6.6 and Charybdis -6.4, Wall Gunner +16.2, Bolas Thrower +12.0 and Torch Runner +7.0 (rows that measure the card they replace), and Rhodian Slingers +6.3, Crystal Guard +6.0, Star Mortar -6.0 (inside their 95% intervals; Rhodian Slingers at 34 HP or 1.7 s read +6.5 / +7.1, so the row measures the Javelineer it replaces). The 64 original cards were never gated in one-age windows (Mammoth Matriarch +46.5, Hunt Cry -45.1 there); their gate is the Standard window. There (n = 400, same seeds) all 64 sat within ±5 on `6d399b1`; now 60 do (mean move 1.6 points): Field Hospital -3.0 → -8.3 and AA Screen -2.0 → -6.5, with Bronze Colossus +5.6 and Sharpshooter +5.2 at the edge. Neither card's numbers changed, and both are already at their static budgets (68% of 70%, 97% of 100%); at 60 gold they read -5.8 and -5.0, so the rows follow the starter power they replace more than the card. Left as they are, open.
- **Mirror (`balance --mode smoke`, Short/Standard/Full):** 110 pass, 27 fail (published: 96 / 27; the 14 new rows are the new powers' static budgets, all pass). The same 27 rows fail as on `main` (power share of kills, value per gold, Standard and Full length windows, age stays, base time to kill), except that Standard first-mover now passes and Standard "army touched by one cast" reads 41.9% (40.0%, limit 40). Short / Standard / Full Final Bell 9.0 / 10.5 / 3.5% (7.0 / 9.5 / 4.0), medians 7:22 / 10:46 / 14:44.
- **Exploits:** 92 pass, 18 fail (published 91 / 19). The turtles still win 0% (band 35-45%, a known ceiling question), bait-wave +0, no-research 35% (Short). New fail: the flag ball in Short War, 49% at n = 100; at n = 400 it reads 46.6% against 43.3% on `main` (limit 45%), a 3-point move inside the noise. A set-ball weight of 1.75 (was 1.5) left it at 46.3%, so the AI is unchanged.
- **Strength:** 45 pass, 4 fail (published 46 / 3): tiers VIII and X tie near 95%, so "rises with every tier" fails in Short (it failed in Standard on `main` too) and tier X beats VIII only 55-58% at n = 40.
- **Forts (smoke):** 114 pass, 53 fail (published 122 / 45). Every "Bell Δ" row compares with the same no-fort mirror, whose Short War Bell rate fell from 12% to 6% at n = 100, so all of them read about 5 points higher together; the fort rows' own Bell rates are equal or lower (Short 5-10% against 8-16%). The per-fort rows (50 pairs, intervals ±12) move both ways.
- **Last Base Standing** 22 / 1 (median 16:17, open since 16:13). **Economy** 15 / 1 (focused War Plan at L7 94 days, open). **Drops** 17 / 17.
- **Gallery e2e fixed:** the dev gallery's art checks flagged nine wave puppets under their A11 height band (the wolves, pup, hound, Swarmling, Hydra, Lindworm and its Bone Wyrm skin, and the Cavalry Picket and Mech Bay levies drawn from a heavy Common). The unit test already exempted the beasts by design; the list now lives in `HEIGHT_BAND_EXEMPT` (`src/visuals/checks.ts`), which `heightBand`, the unit test and the gallery share, with the two heavy-drawn levies added.
- **Sessions (production build, 844x390 and 1280x720, no console errors or failed requests):** new player: first launch, match 1, capsule 1, Army, match 2 by tapping, capsule 2, the forced upgrade, every length open on the picker, a first Ladder win, the Wardrobe Crate, a Long War from Arena 1 (won at 7:22 phone, desktop too), reload to Home. Returning player (Arena 4, 900 trophies, 128 of 208 cards with wave cards in every age): Home, the odds panel with the all-ages line, Army (Cosmic, a card's menu), Album, Profile milestones (Card Scout 100/100, Archivist 128/208), a Medium War with wave cards in the plan, a reload mid-battle (the Retreat toast), a Short Ladder battle to the Result (lost to Captain Kettle on both sizes with the naive tapper), the Sundial capsule, the replay viewer. Granted Jade and Aeon capsules on that save open with 6 and 9 stacks (2,419 and 6,221 Amber), and a two-tap upgrade spends 2 copies.
- **Not changed:** the VS screen shows the "AI General" chip and "AI · Name" for procedural Commanders on purpose (`screens.test.tsx` pins it). Long two-word names wrap to two lines under Army cards on phones and the pool scrolls to show them.

## Every ladder length from Arena 1 (owner decision, 2026-10-03)

- **Rule:** Long War (`full`) and No clock (`last`) are open on the Ladder from Arena 1, together with Short and Standard: arenas 1 and 2 list `['short', 'standard', 'full', 'last']` (`src/content/arenas.ts`, published in b04b9ff). Before, Arena 1 had Short only, Arena 2 Short and Standard, Arena 3 all four. No clock stays out of the Daily, Conquest and the War Path.
- **Defaults:** Home's Battle still plays the shortest timed length (Short War) until the player picks another (`ladderFormat`). Mode select's Ladder card used to start on the longest timed length, which would now drop a new Arena 1 player into a 17½-minute war; it now starts on the same length as Home's Battle (`ladderFormat`).
- **Army and averages:** `reachedAges` and `nextFormat` follow the arena's lengths, so a new player reaches the Long War's 7 ages at once. The starter kit plays every age (the advisor only warns "no Anti-armor yet" in Industrial to Future until those Rares arrive, A3), so a Long War from Arena 1 is never blocked.
- **Rewards (unchanged, for the owner to weigh):** below 400 trophies every timed length pays A6.3's flat +30 trophies and 20 (40) Amber (A15.8), so in Arenas 1-2 the Long War pays no more than the Short War. No clock pays no trophies at every count.
- **Tests:** the gating tests now assert that every length is open from Arena 1. The "locked length names its arena" and fallback rules are still tested on a variant arena table (`mount({ content })` is new in the UI test harness).

## Longer wars pay more from Arena 1; No clock is ranked (owner decision, 2026-10-03)

- **Owner:** "ja, og uden ur skal også give trofæer": a longer battle must pay more trophies than a short one from the very first arena, and No clock (Last Base Standing, `last`) gives trophies too.
- **Rule (data, `src/content/arenas.ts`):** `winByFormat.fromTrophies` 400 → 0, so every ladder win pays its length's row from Arena 1. Wins: Short +30, Standard +36, Full +46, No clock +48 trophies. Amber keeps each row's old Amber-to-trophy ratio (Short 20/26, Standard 27/31, Full and No clock 35/36), rounded: 23 / 31 / 45 / 47 with a Sundial capsule, double without (46 / 62 / 90 / 94). Losses (−20 from 400, never below the arena gate), draws (0), loss protection and the 15 loss/draw Amber are unchanged and now apply to No clock too. `ladder.win` (+30, 20 / 40) remains only for a match without a format and for the tutorial's Amber.
- **No clock ranked:** the `unranked` flag left `LadderWin`, the schema and `isUnranked` (content, meta exports). It already moved the hidden MMR and the loss streak; tests now pin MMR for its win, loss and draw. **Kept:** a Retreat in No clock pays no Amber (A15.4). It still costs the loss's trophies, but below 400 it costs nothing, and with no Final Bell leaving at 1:00 is the only quick way out of a ~16-minute war. `rewards.ts` now keys the rule on the untimed format family.
- **Per minute (60% wins, medians Short 7:39, Medium 10:58, Long 15:21, No clock ~16:13):** from 400 trophies 1.31 / 1.24 / 1.28 / 1.28 trophies a minute; below 400 2.35 / 1.97 / 1.80 / 1.78. Longer wars pay more per match and never more per minute than Short War.
- **UI:** Home's No clock line now shows the win chip (+48 🏆) like the timed lengths; the info sheet's rewards line is built from the same rule meta pays (+48 trophies, 47 Amber, −20 from 400; a Retreat pays no Amber). The Result's "Unranked · stays at N" note and its i18n key are gone; the `lastWin` dev fixture shows +48 / 47. The VS row never mentioned ranking.
- **Economy (`tools/economy.ts`, 30 seeds, before → after):** Amber a day 3,151 → 3,163; Common to max 104 → 104 days, Rare 97 → 98, Epic 64 → 59, Legendary 100 → 99; whole collection 192 → 189; Amber done 135 → 134; War Plan L7 86 → 78; finish gap 57 → 55. Every A6.9 row that passed still passes; the four Phase 3 rows that missed before (all Legendaries 7 days, plan L7, copies done, finish gap) still miss. The A6.9 player plays Short War, so per length a scratch harness (10 seeds) drove the same player at a forced length: Arena 8 (3,400) at 7 matches a day on day 59 → 46 (Short), 44 → 34 (Standard), 35.5 → 24 (Full), never → 22.5 (No clock); at equal play time (~50 min a day: 7 Short, 5 Standard, 3 Full, 3 No clock) 46 / 48 / 55.5 / 51.5 days. DESIGN has no time-to-arena target; time-to-max per length stays within ±10% of Short's.
- **Tool fix:** `EconomyRecorder` counted the 23 unreleased cards (release gate, 6d399b1), which never drop, so every time-to-max read "not reached" on `main`. It now counts released cards only.
- **Tests:** rewards per length at 0, 150, 399, 400, 500 and 3,500 trophies (`meta/test/feats.test.ts`), applyMatchResult per length in Arena 1 and from 400; No clock win / loss / draw / Retreat / loss protection and MMR (`meta/test/lastBase.test.ts`); content rows (`content/test/meta.test.ts`, `lastBase.test.ts`); Home plate and info sheet, Result row (`ui/screens/test/interactions.test.tsx`, `homeMode.test.ts`, `a15.test.tsx`); Short War Amber 23 / 46 in `rewards.test.ts` and `sundial.test.ts`.

## A Retreat gives nothing, in every length (owner decision, 2026-10-03)

- **Owner:** "at give op giver intet": retreating (giving up) pays nothing in every battle length. Before, only No clock (`last`) paid 0 Amber on a Retreat; Short, Standard and Long still paid the 15 loss Amber.
- **What a Retreat grants now (`src/meta/rewards.ts`, any mode):** nothing. No Amber, no Sundial Capsule, no Clay pip (both were already excluded), no feat (an in-battle feat stays findable later, its flag is not set), no quest progress (a "play N battles" quest would otherwise make a 1:00 Retreat a cheap quest step), no Supply Capsule from a banked allowance, and no match-based title (`unlockTitles` gets no match facts; `stats.futureReached` does not count a Retreat). State-based titles the save already earned still unlock, as on any later result. The only staged step of a Ladder Retreat is the trophy row.
- **Kept as a loss:** trophies (−20 from 400, 0 below, never below the arena gate), hidden MMR, the Ladder and War Path loss streaks (loss protection and "try Easy" are help, not rewards), the profile stats (`losses`, `lossesByTier`, `matches`) and `matchesPlayed`. Keeping `matchesPlayed` keeps every gate that reads it (War Plan unlock, quest `fromMatch`, matchmaking seeds) unchanged; the price is that a Retreat landing on a 3rd match makes no Supply Capsule and the allowance waits for the next 3rd match (retired feature, banks only).
- **Never more rewarding than playing on:** the same Ladder battle lost on the field pays 15 Amber, a Sundial claim or a pip, quest progress and feats; the Retreat pays only the identical trophy loss (pinned in `meta/test/retreat.test.ts`).
- **Left battles:** a Ladder battle left after 1:00 is applied at boot as this same Retreat (`src/app/abandon.ts`), so it pays nothing and costs the loss's trophies: a reload is never better or worse than tapping Retreat. The boot notice now says "…counts as a Retreat: a loss with no rewards."
- **Honesty (A15):** Retreat confirm (`ui.pause.retreatBody`): "Retreating counts as a loss and gives no rewards: no Amber, no capsule and no quest progress." The Result shows `ui.result.retreatNoRewards` ("You retreated, so this battle gives no rewards.") at the top of its Rewards column for any Retreat loss, in every mode; the No clock info sheet says "a Retreat gives no rewards". New dev fixture `retreat` (Ladder, left at 1:05, −20).
- **Economy:** the A6.9 economy player never retreats, so `tools/economy.ts` output is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `src/meta/test/retreat.test.ts` (every Ladder length, below 400, field loss vs Retreat, Skirmish / Daily / War Path), `lastBase.test.ts` (timed lengths now 0 Amber), `supplyWarChest.test.ts` (no Supply Capsule, allowance kept), `app/test/abandon.test.ts` (a left battle through real meta pays only the trophies), `ui/screens/test/interactions.test.tsx` (confirm copy, Result line).

## Test profiles behind `?tester=1` (owner request, 2026-10-06)

- **Owner:** a test profile so the owner can skip the tutorial and test the whole game with everything unlocked, on any device and from the live site, hidden from normal players.
- **Rule:** opening the game with `?tester=1` shows the "Test profile" dialog (`src/app/ui/TesterDialog.tsx`, strings in `src/i18n/tester.en.json`): **Everything unlocked**, **Mid-game** or Cancel. A pick asks "This replaces the progress saved in this browser. It cannot be undone."; confirming builds the profile (`src/app/tester.ts`), writes it through the normal save store (validator, checksum, two slots), reads it back, and reloads into Home without `tester=1`. Cancel only drops the flag from the URL. Without the flag nothing changes; nothing in the game links to it.
- **Built at the real time on a real new save:** both profiles start from `meta.newSave` at the current clock (save version, RNG streams, today's quests, Daily day, Sundial period), then add progress; capsules are rolled by meta's normal grant (honest contents, `createdAt` = now) and a final `tickTimers` is a no-op. The onboarding is done (`tutorial.step` 4, the unlock moments, first upgrade and War Plan prompt marked seen) and the release gate runs last, so no unreleased card can appear. The UI fixtures (`midGameSave`, `maxedSave`) are not reused: they import the contract fakes, which the production entry may not reach (`tests/integrity/layers.test.ts`), use a fixed 2026-01-01 epoch and hold capsules with empty contents.
- **Everything unlocked:** every released card at level 9 with the copies for level 10 (so upgrades can be tried), every power, fort, skin, banner, frame, title and cosmetic item, both power slots and the Fort slot open, three auto-filled War Plan presets, the whole War Path beaten on Normal with 3 stars, every Conquest General beaten once (all open), Iron Front (Arena 5) at 1,450 trophies with the Trophy Road claimed below that (the 1,450 node left to claim), hidden MMR in the middle of the arena's AI tiers, Codex Level 60, 500,000 Amber and 100,000 Dust, two capsules of each tier Clay to Aeon (a Trophy Road one that shows its tier and a Sundial one that climbs from Clay), two Wardrobe Crates and a full Sundial.
- **Mid-game:** about three weeks in: Kingsmoat (Arena 3) at 560 trophies (best 610), the starter Commons levelled 6 (Stone) down to 2 (Cosmic), five extra Rares, two Epics and a Legendary, two cards ready to upgrade, the War Path through Bronze L6, three Conquest Generals, the road's powers up to 600, the Fort slot open, 3,450 Amber and 820 Dust, four capsules.
- **Marked:** the save carries `flags['tester.profile']`; Settings (Save panel) and the Profile card show a "Test profile" chip so it is never mistaken for real progress. Settings' Reset leaves it.
- **Unchanged:** no real money and nothing sold; sim, AI, meta, core and content are untouched (the builder lives in the app layer and only calls meta's pure functions).
- **Tests:** `src/app/test/tester.test.ts` (validator, real store round trip, onboarding done and every Home feature open, no unreleased id, timers relative to now, plans valid for every length, a capsule opens, profile contents) and `tests/e2e/tester.spec.ts` (844x390: `/?tester=1`, Everything unlocked, confirm, Home with the mode switcher, all four lengths, War Path and Capsules, then a Long War battle starts; no dialog without the flag, Cancel keeps the save).

## Amber re-tune: Amber becomes a real upgrade constraint (economy lead, owner feedback 2026-10-07)

- **Owner:** "It is as if you can earn money a bit too fast in the game compared to just being able to upgrade everything you want."
- **Measured first** (`tools/economy.ts`, 30 seeds, 365 days; new Amber-gate measures: at the end of each day, after the player upgraded what it could, the Amber that every upgrade the copies already allow would cost). The A6.9 player (7 Short War Ladder matches a day, 60% wins) earned 5,145 Amber and 511 Dust a day (days 11-120) and 240 copies. In the first week nothing waited on Amber on 6 of 7 days and days 0-5 ended with 1,900-3,300 Amber unspent; from day 6 Amber bound for a player who levels all 208 cards (backlog 0.6 / 13.5 / 64.6 days of income on day 7 / 30 / 90), but a player who levels only its 48 War Plan cards (mixed lengths) never met an Amber limit and banked a median 46 days of income (about 230,000 Amber): copies, spread over 208 cards, are what limit the cards you play. The mixed-length player (3 Short, 2 Standard, 1 Long, 1 No clock a day; trophies 30 / 36 / 46 / 48 and the Amber by ratio, a Retreat pays nothing) read the same within a few percent (5,178 Amber a day). Days to War Plan L7: 91.5 (Short) and 87.5 (mixed); median card to max by copies 105.5 / 95 / 59.25 / 104.5 (targets 110 / 101 / 69 / 112), at L10 with Amber paid 158.75 / 172.5 / 177.75 / 172. First week: no starter card reaches L10 in 7 or 30 days (copy-gated; mean starter level L3.5 on day 7, the first War Plan L3.67).
- **Where Amber came from** (days 0-120): capsules 4,540 a day (87%), quests 325, matches 220, Trophy Road 100, Codex Levels 35. So the lever is capsule Amber; match Amber (by length), quests, the road, Codex Levels, the War Path, upgrade costs, Dust rates and copies are unchanged.
- **Rule (data, `src/content/capsules.ts`; DESIGN A6.4):** capsule Amber ×0.8 on the base table (Arenas 1-2 and the onboarding script): Clay 85, Bronze 170, Silver 425, Jade 1,120, Gold 2,110, Platinum 2,240, Aeon 2,880 (was 105 / 210 / 530 / 1,400 / 2,640 / 2,800 / 3,600); ×0.65 on the all-ages table (Arena 3 and up): 120 / 235 / 595 / 1,570 / 2,965 / 3,145 / 4,045 (was 181 / 363 / 916 / 2,419 / 4,562 / 4,838 / 6,221). Per bag capsule 462.0 Amber (710.7) and 330.3 on the base table (411.3). The Age Capsule follows the Silver Amber. Both tables still rise up the ladder and no all-ages value is below the base table (schema).
- **Tried and rejected** (10 seeds each): a deeper cut (all-ages ×0.5-0.55) left the War-Plan-only player still never Amber-blocked (bank 18-23 days of income) while the collection slipped to about a year; steeper upgrade costs (L2-L10 +4% to +16%) did the same as an income cut but made days 0-1 Amber-blocked, so the first session felt tight; keeping the base table whole only moved the first constrained day from day 2 to day 3, so the small ×0.8 there just trims the onboarding lump (day 0 now ends with 650 Amber spare instead of 1,900, and nothing waiting). Binding the 48-card player would take about a 70% cut and push the whole collection past 18 months, so it was not done: the cards you play stay copy-gated by design (A6.9 per-card targets), and Amber decides what else you level.
- **After** (30 seeds): Amber 3,565 a day (−31%), copies 238 (unchanged), Dust 470; Amber-blocked days 100% in days 11-120 and 57% in days 0-6 (4 of 7; days 0-1 leave nothing waiting); backlog 3.6 / 34.7 / 127.2 days of income on day 7 / 30 / 90, so from day 2 the player regularly chooses which card to upgrade; first War Plan L3.62 on day 7 (3.67); median card to max by copies 106 / 99.5 / 59 / 103.5 (all within ±20% of 110 / 101 / 69 / 112), at L10 with Amber paid 233.25 / 258.25 / 270 / 262.5; War Plan L7 89 days (91.5; open Phase 3 row, as before); all Legendaries day 22 (21); copies done day 226.5, Amber done day 313, whole collection 313 days (10.3 months; was 228). Mixed-length player: 3,590 Amber a day, week-1 blocked days 29% → 71%, backlog day 7 0.7 → 3.7 days, collection 221.5 → 307, plan L7 87.5 → 94.5. War-Plan-only player: plan maxed day 212.5 and L7 day 95.5 either way, bank 46 → 31 days of income (230,000 → 107,000 Amber). Casual player (3 a day, 730 days): copy times unchanged (198.25 / 164.25 / 109.25 / 184.5), collection 410.5 → 524 days. `drops --mode smoke` 17 of 17.
- **Targets changed (A6.9, stricter, per the owner's feedback):** Amber per bag capsule 710 → 460, Amber a day ~5,150 → ~3,600, whole collection ~7-7.5 → ~10 months (Amber-gated), and the finish rule "copies and Amber within 30 days" becomes "Amber finishes 30-120 days after the copies". New gates in `tools/economy.ts`: at least 75% of days 11-120 end with a copy-ready upgrade the Amber cannot pay, the day-7 backlog is 1-7 days of income (Amber matters in the first week without being stingy), and the first War Plan's day-7 mean level is at least L3.3. Per-card time-to-max targets (copies) are unchanged. `economy`: 18 pass, 1 fail (the open War Plan L7 row), 8 info.
- **Tool:** `tools/economy.ts` gains the Amber-gate, L10 and first-week measures (`amberBacklog`, `EconomyRecorder.gate` / `starter`), a `formats` model option (`--formats mixed` or a list; `MIXED_FORMATS`), a `focusAges` option for a player who levels only its War Plan's first N ages (used for the War-Plan-only figures), and No clock's match length (16:15) for quest stats.
- **Tests:** `src/content/test/meta.test.ts` (both Amber columns, 330.3 and 462.025 per bag capsule), `src/meta/test/newSave.test.ts` (33,030 centi-Amber), `tools/test/economy.test.ts` (the backlog climbs as far as copies reach, blocked days, backlog in days of income, bank, first week, the new gates fail when loose or stingy).

## The years-long Amber curve: save up for the endgame (economy lead, owner decision 2026-10-07)

- **Owner (translated from Danish):** "Tighten Amber, maybe not quite that hard [not the full 70% cut], but you should not just be able to afford upgrading everything all the time; you must save up. The long-term intent is that the game can be played for years, like Clash of Clans or Clash Royale, so there are always new goals to fight for."
- **Measured first** (`tools/economy.ts`, 30 seeds; the tool now also runs a War-Plan-only player, `focusAges` 8, for 548 days, and a casual twin). After the first Amber re-tune, the player who levels only its War Plan never met the Amber gate: 0% of days 10-364 ended with a copy-ready upgrade it could not pay, its median bank was 24.5 days of income (30.8 with mixed lengths), an upgrade to L10 cost 0.5 days of income and its whole War Plan reached L10 on day 214 (copy-gated). The collector (all 208 cards) maxed everything on day 313. Amber income is about 3,550 a day in days 11-120 and falls to about 3,000 once the Trophy Road and the early Codex Levels are behind.
- **Rule (data, `src/content/rarities.ts`; DESIGN A6.6):** Amber per level L2-L10 = 20 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 350 / **800 / 1,700 / 4,800 / 12,000** (was 550 / 800 / 1,200 / 1,700 for L7-L10); a card from L1 to L10 costs 20,020 Amber (4,970). L2-L6, income, copies, capsule tables, odds, pity and every other Amber source are unchanged. The level cap stays 10, so no new system was needed: the existing table carries the long horizon.
- **Why the top of the curve and not income:** the War-Plan-only player is over-supplied by about 5 times (it banked months of income), and an income cut deep enough to bind it (about 70%) would make the first week and every early level slow. A steeper top binds only where the endgame lives: in the first week the War-Plan-only player still has nothing waiting on Amber (0% of days 0-6, first War Plan L3.53 on day 7), and the gate starts as its cards reach L6-L7. No further income trim was needed.
- **Tried** (4-6 seeds each; War-Plan-only plan maxed / collection / L10 in days of income): totals 24,400-24,700 with a steeper middle (L5 500-700) made days 0-6 tight for the collector (backlog on day 7 7.6-11.4 days of income, over the 1-7 gate) and pushed the War Plan to 16-17 months and the collection to 4.3-4.7 years; [.., 350, 750, 1,600, 4,500, 11,000] (18,570) gave 12.2-12.5 months and 3.4-3.5 years, too close to the 12-month floor; [.., 350, 800, 1,800, 5,000, 12,000] (20,320) 13.7 months and 3.8 years. The shipped 20,020 sits in the middle.
- **After** (30 seeds, 7 Short War matches a day): Amber 3,561 a day, copies 237 (unchanged); median card to max by copies 104.5 / 98 / 60.25 / 103.5 days (targets 110 / 101 / 69 / 112); all Legendaries day 21; War Plan L7 94 (open Phase 3 row, as before); copies done day 223.5; collector Amber-blocked days 11-120 100%, days 0-6 71% (57%), day-7 backlog 5.5 days of income (3.6), first War Plan L3.58 on day 7 (3.62). **War-Plan-only player:** Amber-blocked on 94% of days 10-364 (0%), 81% of days 11-120, 0% of days 0-6; median bank 0.8 days of income (24.5); an upgrade to L10 costs a median 4.0 days of income when bought (0.5), the dearest upgrade 5.8 days (0.8); whole War Plan at L10 on day 410.5, 13.5 months (214). **Collection:** 1,366 days projected (3.7 years; 313 before), confirmed by a 1,600-day run (4 seeds: 1,372); the collector's own War Plan maxes after about 790 days, because it spends between the plan's large upgrades; its median card reaches L10 after 799 / 988 / 1,069 / 1,010 days. **Mixed lengths:** collection 1,341 days projected (307), War-Plan-only plan 403.5 days (212.5), blocked days 10-364 93% (0%), first War Plan L3.77 on day 7. **Casual player (3 a day):** copy times unchanged (198 / 167 / 109.5 / 186.5), collection 2,273 days projected (6.2 years; 2,281 measured over 2,500 days, 3 seeds; 524 before), War-Plan-only plan maxed on day 687 (1.9 years; 391), dearest upgrade 10.7 days of income (1.5), so every level stays within two weeks even at 3 matches a day.
- **Long-term targets (A6.9, gated in `tools/economy.ts`):** War-Plan-only player: whole active War Plan at L10 in 12-18 months, at least 75% of days 10-364 Amber-blocked, a median upgrade to L10 of at least 3 days of income, no upgrade above 14 days of income. Collector: whole collection about 3.5 years ±20% (was about 10 months), Amber finishing at least 30 days after the copies (was 30-120). The brief's example of 2-3 years for the collection cannot be met together with a 12-18 month War Plan by one cost curve: income is flat in the long run and the collection has about 3.3 times the War Plan's cards (208 against up to 64), so the War Plan target was kept and the collection lands at about 3.7 years. Every new content wave adds goals on top: each released card adds 20,020 Amber, about a week of income.
- **The War Plan is up to 64 cards, not 48:** the 48 starter troops fill the troop slots, and each new turret goes into an empty turret slot of its age when it drops (`fillEmptySlot`, A6.4), so the War-Plan-only player levels up to 16 turrets too. The War Plan rows measure the active plan as it is on each day.
- **Side effect, open question for the owner: Dust.** Copies past L10 turn into Dust only when a card reaches L10, and cards now reach L10 much later, so Dust drops from 470 to 176 a day in days 11-120 and from 1.40 million to 53,000 in the first year; it arrives later, in lumps, as cards max. Dust prices, crafting and skins are unchanged. A possible follow-up (a rule change in `src/meta`, not built): turn the copies a card can never use (more than it still needs for L10, counting unopened capsules) into Dust at reveal time, so Dust income returns to about its old level without touching Amber. Not decided here.
- **Possible later goals (proposals only, nothing built):** (1) keep releasing content waves: each card adds a new Amber goal, and a wave of 24 cards adds about 5 months for a collector; (2) a post-L10 Mastery track per card (cosmetic stars, a frame or a foil step paid with Dust or Amber after L10, no stat gain, so the fixed level cap and fairness stay) for players who maxed their War Plan; (3) season-style Trophy Road resets with cosmetic rewards. Each needs an owner decision and a design pass; the cost table carries the next years without them.
- **Kept:** onboarding and the forced first upgrade (L2 still 20 Amber), the first-week feel (first War Plan L3.58 on day 7, gate L3.3), copies time-to-max per card, the Sundial, the bag, the odds and pity, the A6.3 match Amber by length and a Retreat paying nothing; no real money, nothing sold.
- **Tool:** `tools/economy.ts` gains the War-Plan-only run (gated by `planChecks`: `economy.plan.blocked`, `.topUpgrade`, `.maxUpgrade`, `.maxed`) and its casual twin (reported), per-upgrade cost in days of income at the time (`EconomyRecorder.upgrade`, `upgradeCost`), the active War Plan's max day (`planMaxDay`), days 10-364 blocked (`blockedShareYear`) and projected finish dates past the run (`amberDoneProjected`, `collectionMaxedProjected`, from the last 60 days of income); `sim-cli economy --plan-days N` (0 skips the War-Plan-only runs). `economy` (Short and mixed): every gate passes except the open War Plan L7 row.
- **Tests:** `src/content/test/meta.test.ts` (20,020 a card, L2-L6 unchanged), `src/meta/test/upgrades.test.ts` and `newSave.test.ts` (the new row), `tools/test/economy.test.ts` (projection, upgrade cost in days of income, the active War Plan's max day, `planChecks` passing on target and failing loose or stingy, the collector's years-long target).

## UI art pass, Track A (Home, War Path, backdrops, nav and Battle icons) (2026-10-07)

- **Owner:** the menus look homemade; named the War Path hover, the castle on Home, the crooked swords, the stick-figure War Path, the arc birds. Layout stays.

Canvas 2D, no Pixi; the procedural puppet stays the fallback; Home cross-fades the picture in (200 ms).
- **Colour rules as code:** `src/ui/components/tone.ts` implements the art sheet (shadow × 0.74 matte / × 0.65 metal, warm hues 8° toward red, highlight 32% / 60% toward white or warm gold, outline fill × 0.40 with value ≤ 0.38). Icons use `Part` (fill, clipped band, clipped highlight, own outline); map and Home props use `Cel`. Every other icon got per-fill outlines instead of the fixed `#1b1330`.

- **War Path card (audit #8):** the hover bug (bare `scale()` replacing the centring translate) is gone: the card lifts (−4 px, 1.02, e5 shadow), three art layers pan at 1x/1.5x/2.25x (far range, the region's hero set piece and a grove, the road with a glossy node and the Standard Bearer), a glint crosses, press squashes to 0.97. The entrance animation no longer holds its transform (`backwards`), so hover and press can move the card. Reduce motion: border glow and brightness only.

It travels through `portrait` with the card id `strip:<clip>:<card>` because the kit's wiring files are not Track A's (request `docs/requests/uiart-a-sprite-strip-kit.md`). The map bearer is the Bronze Standard Bearer's sheet: idle on its node, walk clip facing its way during the 450 ms ceremony move.
- **Home diorama (audit #3):** a wobbly island top with a shaded back rim and lit front lip, a worn lane with ruts and edge stones, three rock strata with a shaded right face, swaying roots, three floating chunks bobbing out of phase, outlines in each fill's own dark; all eight landmarks redrawn with `Cel` (brick courses, framed lit windows that flicker, shaded smoke puffs). Sky: soft sun with faint rays, cumulus clouds (flat shaded undersides) at three depths. The prop kit's groves and rocks dress the rim.
- **Birds (audit #5):** a real bird (body, head, beak, tail, near and far wing) in five flap frames plus a glide pose, kinds by age (crow, gull, pigeon, hover-drone, star-manta). UI: `Bird`/`Flock` (loose V, staggered phases, banking flock path); Home flocks, War Path flocks and the tap scatter use it. Battle: parts `bd.bird|drone|manta.f0-f5`, frame-swapped in the backdrop's drift step (90 ms a frame, glides now and then), Future and Cosmic get drones and mantas, Lite keeps half the flocks, reduce motion glides at half speed.

## Owner decision 2026-10-07: spare copies turn into Dust when the capsule opens (economy, WP7, WP10)

- **Owner:** since the steep L7-L10 Amber costs, Dust income fell sharply in year one (about 470 → 176 a day) because surplus copies only turned into Dust at L10. Decided: copies a card can never use (beyond what it still needs to reach max level, counting the copies it already holds) convert to Dust when the capsule is opened, shown honestly in the capsule summary.
- **Rule (meta, `src/meta/capsules/open.ts`, `copiesStillNeeded` in `src/meta/economy.ts`):** at reveal each stack keeps `min(copies, copies the card still needs for L10)`; the rest pays `dustPerExtraCopy` each into the stack's `dust` (a card at L10 still converts every copy). The stack keeps its rolled `copies` (the reveal shows what the capsule held); the copies bar comes from the collection before and after, so it shows what was kept. Counting only owned copies at open time is enough: each later capsule sees the copies the earlier ones added. No save migration: copies a save already holds above the line stay and convert at L10 as before (`upgrades.ts`). No contract change.
- **Shown:** the summary item reads "Spare copies: +N Dust" when a card kept some copies (still "+N Dust" for a maxed card); the reveal fan's chip flies "+kept · +N Dust" into the bar; the odds panel states the rule (`ui.odds.spareCopies`). The capsule bench gains a `dust-spare` case (4 of 14 copies kept). Checked at 844x390: the line fits the summary card under the name, above the 35/35 bar.
- **Crafting stays coherent:** a card that already holds every copy L10 needs cannot be crafted (`craft` → `copiesFull`; the Card screen hides "Craft a copy" via `UpgradeState.copiesFull`), since the copy would only turn back into Dust.
- **Measured** (`tools/economy.ts`, 30 seeds, 365 days; HEAD copy for before): Dust a day in days 11-120 / in the first year: **176 / 52,960** today; with the rule at the old rates (5 / 20 / 100 / 400) **2,143 / 1.39 million**, i.e. the same yearly total as before the years-long curve (1.40 million), but from the first weeks. That would buy every craftable skin and collection item (35 skins for 45,000 Dust and 197 items for 40,390) in about six weeks and let a player craft ~20 Rare copies a day for its War Plan while copies still matter. **Tried:** rates ÷5 (1 / 4 / 20 / 80) 559 a day (10 seeds); **shipped 1 / 3 / 15 / 60: 476 a day, 262,000 in the first year** (30 seeds), back on the ~470 the owner remembered. Every other economy row is unchanged (copies, Amber, the War-Plan-only and collector curves, the open War Plan L7 row), because only Dust moved.
- **Target (A6.9, gated in `tools/economy.ts` as `economy.dustPerDay`):** ~470 Dust a day in days 11-120 ± 20%; the year's total reported. Crafting (40 / 100 / 400 / 1,600) and skin prices (200 / 800 / 3,000; duplicate skins 50 / 200 / 800) unchanged: a Legendary crate skin is about a week of Dust, a crafted copy costs 27-40 spare copies' worth.
- **Tests:** `src/meta/test/open.test.ts` (partial conversion, everything spare once full, a new card's surplus, the shown capsule Dust), `upgrades.test.ts` (`copiesFull`), `newSave.test.ts` (`copiesStillNeeded`), `src/content/test/meta.test.ts` (rates), `tools/test/economy.test.ts` (the Dust gate).

## Owner decision 2026-10-07: Heavy a bit overpowered (balance, WP1)

- **Owner:** Heavy is a bit overpowered. Its strength is HP, so HP stays; fix it with slightly lower Heavy damage and/or higher Anti-heavy damage against Heavy. Targets: Heavy still beats Infantry, Anti-heavy wins equal-gold duels against Heavy clearly (about 70-80%), the Heavy share of winning armies down noticeably, the Heavy-spam proxy inside its gate, no Final Bell or match-length regression, touched cards within ±5.
- **Measured first** (HEAD `633e433`, content `cba469d0`; L7; scratch harnesses on `src/sim` and `tools/lib`, kept in the session scratchpad; `sim-cli exploits`). Equal-gold duels on the real sim (M = 50 + half the HP-left difference; 2 Heavies against 6 Infantry, 4 Ranged, 3 Support, 4 Long range; 3 and 6 Anti-heavy against 2 and 4 Heavies; all 16 Heavy Commons against every Infantry Common and both Anti-heavy cards of their age): Heavy beats the starter Infantry 65-84 in seven ages (Gunpowder 42 and 50: Corsairs beat both Gunpowder Heavies, a known gap), beats Ranged 69-84 and Support 50-95; every age's starter Anti-heavy wins 3 v 2 at 68-87 and 6 v 4 at 62-90 (mean over all 16 Anti-heavy cards 78.3), so the "around 70-80%" target already held, with two exceptions: the Wall Gunner loses to both Gunpowder Heavies (31-36) and the Assault Gun's splash holds the Modern Anti-heavies to 66-68 (Sticky Bomber 6 v 4: 32). Heavy-spam: mono Heavy wins 0% against tier VII in Short and Standard and 0-2.5% in every one-age lane; mono Anti-heavy beats mono Heavy 95-100% in every age. Balanced bot mirrors (baseline plan, tiers V / VII / X, 200 each): the winner's armies hold 28.6-30.6% Heavy gold against the loser's 19.7-22.0%, and the side with the larger Heavy share wins 74.0-81.4%.
- **The Heavy share of winning armies is mostly the winner's bank.** With the same seeds a tier VII bot whose plan has no Heavy at all beats the baseline plan 56.5% (Short) and 65.5% (Standard), and one with a second Heavy also wins 58.5 / 59.5%; a bot that is ahead simply has the 150 gold for Heavies more often. Patches without a counter regeneration moved nothing: Heavy damage −10% left winners at 28.9-29.9% and the larger-Heavy side at 67.8-80.5%; Anti-heavy ×3.0 → ×3.3 left 29.0-30.3% and 70.6-82.9%.
- **Breakpoints decide the duels.** Heavy damage changes the outcome only where it changes the hits needed: −3% on the Tuskback (42 → 41) turns the Torch Runner duel from 54 to 33, −7% (39) the Bonker duel from 76 to 50; −10% on every Heavy dropped Heavy against Infantry to 14-73. Anti-heavy damage barely moves (the Heavies' high HP needs the same number of hits either way).
- **Rule (data, `src/content/raw/<age>.ts`; DESIGN A2.6 "Heavy trim"):** Heavy Common damage −2 to −5% wherever no Infantry Common of the age needs an extra hit: War Chariot 49 → 48, War Elephant 40 → 38, Destrier Knight 57 → 54, Greatsword Knight 42 → 40, Cuirassier 76 → 74, Dragoon 52 → 49, Steam Golem 89 → 85, Steam Tractor 66 → 65, Tankette 104 → 99, Assault Gun 88 → 84, Crab Mech 112 → 109, Hover Tank 188 → 179, Asteroid Golem 150 → 146. HP, cost, speed and the Gore / charge bonuses unchanged. Kept: Tuskback 42 and Walker Mech 140 (one point less flips a breakpoint), Woolly Rhino 33 (at 31 its per-card row rose from +1.8 to +8.9: the counter regeneration made the bots field it differently). The Anti-heavy multipliers were left alone: they already win clearly, and a raise also hits the 16 armored or mech Epics. Counters regenerated (input hash `71a1ef3f`).
- **After** (same seeds): duels Heavy vs the starter Infantry unchanged per age (65-83, Gunpowder 42 / 50), starter Anti-heavy 3 v 2 69-88 and 6 v 4 62-91 (mean over all 16 cards 78.7), 1 v 1 27-63. Per-card rows of the touched wave Heavies (one-age windows, n = 400): War Elephant −1.8 → −3.1, Greatsword Knight +1.5 → +1.8, Dragoon −0.5 → −2.5, Steam Tractor +3.1 → +3.0, Assault Gun −4.3 → −4.5, Crab Mech −2.5 → −2.0, Asteroid Golem +0.1 → −0.4 (the eight starter Heavies are part of the control and have no row); the Anti-heavy rows moved −2.4 to +0.6 (Sticky Bomber −3.9 → −6.0, Antimatter Rifler −2.4 → −4.4; Wall Gunner +16.3 → +16.1 as before). Mirrors: winners' Heavy share 28.5-31.2% (28.6-30.6%), the larger-Heavy side wins 72.7-78.9% (74.0-81.4%; mean 75.5 against 77.8). A plan with a second Heavy is worth less than before (it beats the baseline plan 56.5 / 53.5%, was 58.5 / 59.5%) and one with none more (58.0 / 70.0%, was 56.5 / 65.5%). Balance mirror (`balance --mode smoke`, 200 per format): Short 7:23 → 7:28 with the Final Bell 10.0 → 10.0%, Standard 10:43 → 10:38 with the Bell 12.0 → 8.5% (29 pass, 10 fail; 28 / 11 before, the same rows).Heavy-spam (same exploit run): mono Heavy 0 / 0% against tier VII before and after and 0-2.5% in every one-age lane; mono Anti-heavy beats mono Heavy 100% in every age except Gunpowder (95 → 80%, gate ≥ 70%).
- **Not met, for the owner:** the Heavy share of winning armies did not fall noticeably (it is the winner's bank, see above), and no damage or multiplier change small enough to keep "Heavy beats Infantry" moves it. The levers that would are not numbers on the Heavy card: how often the bots buy Heavies (the AI plan, A7.2), or the Heavy's price. Also open: the Wall Gunner still loses to both Gunpowder Heavies in equal-gold duels (31-36) while its per-card row reads +16 (it measures the Grenadier it replaces), and Gunpowder's Corsairs beat both Gunpowder Heavies (42 / 50).

## Owner decision 2026-10-07: Long range units shoot clearly further (balance, WP1, WP12 tools)

- **Owner:** extra-long-range units are too weak. Give the Long range Rare of every age (and other long-range wave cards with the arc or the H6 trait) clearly longer range, so a battle forms layers (tanks in front, infantry in the middle, long range at the back) instead of one clump. Keep them slow-firing, ground only, half damage to bases, with a minimum range; tune damage or rate so each card stays within ±5 and the turtle and camping proxies do not improve.
- **Scope:** the eight Long range Rares (Atlatl Thrower, Cretan Archer, Yeoman Archer, Coehorn Crew, Trench Mortar, Mortar Team, Arc Lobber, Star Mortar). No other wave card carries the H6 trait; the arc Epics (Mangonel Cart, Bronze Cannon) are siege and artillery cards with their own role and were left alone.
- **Range (data; DESIGN A5.1):** +50 to +80: Stone 320 → 400, Bronze 330 → 410, Medieval 350 → 430, Gunpowder 360 → 440, Industrial 370 → 440, Modern 380 → 440, Future 380 → 440, Cosmic 390 → 440, about twice the Ranged Commons' 200-270. The cap of 440 keeps a Long range unit that shells a base inside the Rare arc turrets' 480 even with Long Draw (+30); the old "at least 90 lu inside" rule becomes "inside, with Long Draw". Arc speed (300 lu/s), min range 90, splash r35, ground only and half damage to bases are unchanged.
- **Tuning** (per-card rows, one-age windows, n = 400, tier V Balanced, L7, before → after): with range alone (a `--patch` run) Cretan Archer, Trench Mortar, Mortar Team and Arc Lobber rose 11-13 points. Every counter regeneration moved them again by several points (the bots' counter scores include range), so the final values were measured after the last regeneration: Atlatl Thrower +1.1 → +1.8 (2.6 s kept), Cretan Archer −2.1 → +3.9 (2.4 → 2.5 s), Yeoman Archer −3.0 → −0.5 (2.6 s kept), Coehorn Crew +3.9 → +4.4 (2.6 → 3.0 s), Trench Mortar −0.4 → +3.9 (2.6 → 3.4 s), Mortar Team −2.6 → +1.0 (2.6 → 3.0 s), Arc Lobber −3.9 → −4.1 (2.6 → 2.9 s; 2.8 s read +7.1 and 3.0 s −8.5), Star Mortar −6.0 → −5.75 (2.6 → 2.8 s; it read −13.5 at 2.6 s with the range alone and +12.9 with 210 damage, so it stays where it was, just outside the band as in the release check). Damage, HP and base damage unchanged.
- **Proxies (built, `tools/proxies.ts`; DESIGN A5.1 had named them):** `mono_longrange` (only the Long range card, set up like `mono_heavy`; gate ≤ 35%) and `longrange_turtle` (the 4-turret Hold turtle with the Long range card in its Ranged slot, Long Draw and Watchtowers first; the turtle band, so it is in `TURTLES`), both in `EXPLOIT_PROXIES`; `isLongRange`, `longRangePlan` and the `longRange` mono group. Before → after (`exploits --mode smoke`, 100 per format vs tier VII, same seeds): `mono_longrange` 0 / 1% → 3 / 3% (Short / Standard; gate ≤ 35%, inside the noise), `longrange_turtle` 0 / 0% → 0 / 0%, and the turtle family (`turret_turtle`, `tech_turtle`, `fallback_turtle`, `home_turtle`) 0% before and after in both formats, so camping behind turrets with them did not improve. The one row that moved more: `mass_splash` (whose plan picks up the Long range cards, they splash) 7 → 20% in Short (gate ≤ 55%; Standard unchanged). Whole run: 72 pass, 13 fail (70 / 15 before; the flag ball, 46 → 45%, and few-then-evolve vs tier IV, 12 → 9%, now pass; the same turtle-band and bait rows fail).
- **Bots, no change needed:** a ranged unit stops at its range and melee walks past parked ranged units (A2.7), so Long range units settle behind their own front. Probe (tier VII mirrors with the Long range card in every Ranged slot, 20 matches per format, once a second): distance behind the own front p25 / p50 / p75 Short 101 / 281 / 335 → 69 / 277 / 405 lu, Standard 35 / 212 / 354 → 29 / 236 / 418 lu; the share of seconds a Long range unit is the front unit 14.7 → 14.0% (Short) and 16.3 → 18.6% (Standard; mostly a lone survivor); shot distance p50 338 → 380 and 315 → 368 lu.
- **View, no change needed:** the landing marker and the parabola already come from `projectileFired`; at 844x390 the follow camera frames the clash and both Long range lines sit at the screen edges with their arcs crossing the frame, at 1280x720 the three layers read clearly (sandbox screenshots in the session scratchpad `shots/`).
- **Kept open:** the Star Mortar's row (−5.75, −6.0 before) and the old turtle band (every turtle wins 0%, a known ceiling question).

## Owner request 2026-10-07: a satisfying explosion when the rarest cards come out (WP10 capsule, WP6 audio)

- **Built (DESIGN A10 step 5a, A12, A13):** a new plan step `rarityBurst` right after every Rare, Epic or Legendary pre-signal, before the flip or the walkout, scaled with the rarity: Rare a small pop (0.4 s), Epic a strong burst (0.7 s), Legendary a big explosion (1.0 s). The pre-signal is the anticipation (tremble, light leaking from the edges and from cracks spreading out of the medallion, light drawn in, the new `rarity_riser` pitched by the plan to peak on the pop); then a short windup, the pop (light, streaks, sparks, shards, puffs and glints in the rarity colour, 1-3 shockwave rings, a tinted flash, a brief view-only hit-stop, trauma), the card blasted out and slamming back into place with a squash and bounce, embers drifting up; Epic bolts crackle, a Legendary leaves gold leaf. Every number lives in `src/capsule/rarityBurst.ts` and grows with the rarity (tested).
- **Placement:** before the identity reveal, not on the face landing, so one rule fits every rarity: the burst shows the rarity, the flip (or the Legendary walkout, which must keep its own reveal) shows who it is. The face-down card keeps its back lit; the existing flip snap stays as the smaller second beat.
- **Honesty:** the burst only presents a rarity the card's own pre-signal already shows; `checkPlan` rejects a burst out of place, at a different rarity or missing; tests run the show normal, held, tapped and skipped and with Quick reveal and check the reveal, cards and summary stay identical. It never plays for Common, and never hints at anything that did not happen.
- **Settings:** Reduce motion has no shake, flash, hit-stop, punch, blast, bounce, rings or tremble: a calm glow, a few soft motes and slow embers, and the sound. Lite halves particles and embers and keeps at most two rings (burst particles capped at 160 / 80 / 12). Skip passes over it silently; hold fast-forwards it, a tap hurries it. Quick reveal, Open all (Epic and up) and the Wardrobe Crate get it too.
- **Audio:** four new ids in the `capsule` sheet, rendered by `tools/audio/sfx/sounds_rarity_burst.py` (ZzFX fallbacks in `src/audio/sounds.ts`): `rarity_riser`, `rarity_burst_rare`, `rarity_burst_epic`, `rarity_burst_legendary` (boom, shimmer, and a choir-and-brass sting for Legendary).
- **Bench:** a "Rarity burst" group (`burst-rare`, `burst-epic`, `burst-legendary`, `burst-ladder`), all with Quick reveal so the cards come at once.

## Owner request 2026-10-07: Customize tap-to-equip (WP9)

- **Rule:** no Equip button on any item card. A tap on an owned card equips it with the existing feedback (stamp sound, the tile pops, a toast with Undo); a tap on the equipped one does nothing. Only the chosen item shows its state ("Equipped", or "In wheel" for emotes and quotes) as a flat pill with the check badge. Locked items keep their short how-earned line and Dust price. Every Customize tab, plus troop skins in the Collection and Card detail's skin picker, follow the same pattern.

## Owner request 2026-10-07: saved battle decks A/B/C (WP9)

- **Placement:** the deck switch replaces the old "A ▾" dropdown at the top of Army, left of the age tabs (three named segments, gold edge, cards icon); the header's Card Album count button went to make room for it (the Album stays one tap away in Locked; the band's height budget at 844 × 390 had no room for a second row). Home's battle plate shows the same switch under the length line, next to Battle. Both are secondary controls: Battle stays the one primary.
- **Behaviour:** a tap plays that deck at once (the army you look at is the army you play; Army flips the band in and clears Undo and any selection). The deck in use carries a pencil; its sheet renames it (16 characters) and copies another saved deck into it ("Copy from", keeps its own name, deep copies the loadouts, Undo restores). A deck never saved starts as a copy of the deck in use under its default letter (B, C), stored in order because `meta.setWarPlan` refuses gaps; before, a new preset started empty and auto-fill filled it with starter Commons at match time, which is playable but surprising.
- **Unlock:** right after the onboarding (`tutorial.step >= 4`), for any legacy save, and for any save that already holds more than one deck; before, the presets waited for the first boss (10 wins).
- **Hint:** "New: 3 decks. Tap one to switch." once, wherever the switch is first seen, as a first-seen caption like the others (MR-28): it shows for 5 s (as the No clock caption) and counts as seen (`flags['ui-seen.decks']`) once it has shown in full or the player taps a deck; it is a note, not a button (taps pass through it, and it is no undersized target); on Home it waits while an unlock moment, a currency caption or the No clock caption is due (one at a time, U8). In Army it hangs under the switch, so the header lifts above the In battle band while it shows.
- **Long names:** on the plate the segments start from their names' width and share the rest, so renamed decks keep their whole names on a phone ("Legends · Classic · Air" fits at 800 × 360; the UI budget test caught the first, equal-width version clipping them); only names too long together fall back to an ellipsis. In Army a segment takes 52-112 px.
- **Save:** no migration: names and `activePlan` were already in the save, and decks are added only through `setWarPlan`.

## Owner request 2026-10-07: Retreat at once (WP1 content, WP11 app, WP9)

- **Rule:** `economy.retreatAfterMs` 60,000 → **0** (the schema allows 0; `null` still means no Retreat, the tutorial). The Pause menu shows Retreat from the first second with no lock or "Retreat unlocks at 1:00" tooltip; the confirm and the Result say it is a loss with no rewards.
- **Reload or abandon:** a Ladder battle is marked open (`ageborn.openMatch.v1`) on its first simulated tick after the start countdown, so a reload at any later moment counts as a Retreat on the next boot (a loss with no rewards, the trophies of a loss). Before, the first minute was a free void window. **Other modes keep the void rule** (War Path, Conquest, Daily, Quick Battle, Skirmish): their Retreat already moves no trophies and pays nothing, so a reload there gains nothing; the Ladder is the only mode where a loss costs something to dodge.
- **Sim:** the retreat check reads `retreatTick`; with 0 ms it compiles to the first tick, so the sim code is unchanged (no `SIM_VERSION` change, see the stance entry for the content hash).

## Owner request 2026-10-07: no stance cooldown (WP1 content, WP7 AI, WP9 HUD, WP12 tools)

- **Rule:** Back, Hold and Charge switch instantly. `economy.stanceCooldownMs` 3,000 → **0**; the schema allows 0, which compiles to the B3 minimum of one tick (`msToTicks` clamps to 1), so a change is accepted on every tick. A flag move keeps its 1 s limit. The HUD lost its wait sweep and the "Ready in 2s" denial (`src/ui/hud/Stance.tsx`, `model.ts`, `hud.css`, `hud.stanceWait`).
- **Determinism:** no sim code changed, so `SIM_VERSION` stays and no golden moves (the goldens use the frozen fixture tables, stance 2,000 ms, retreat 60,000 ms). The live content hash changes `7af6a652` → `060e7fc4` (the snapshot in `src/content/test/compile.test.ts`), and `counters.json` was regenerated for its input hash only (the matrix is identical: 0 of 12,720 pairs changed).
- **Bots never flicker:** the AI keeps its own gap between stance commands, `AI_STANCE_GAP_TICKS` 60 (3 s) in `src/ai/book.ts`, the larger of that and the content cooldown, read by the ledger; the brain's 8 s dwell is unchanged. Every General plays exactly as before: Balanced vs tier IV is identical before and after (36.0 / 41.8%, Short / Standard, n = 400, same seeds).
- **Exploit measurement (headless, `tools/proxies.ts`, 400 games per format, draws half, L7):** a new proxy `stance_dancer` (Fall back the moment its front fights, Charge again at once, checked every 0.1 s, never sends the stance it already has) and the existing `stance_toggler` (now allowed a 0.5 s gap), each with the old 3 s cooldown (`--patch`) and without it:
  - `stance_dancer` vs the Balanced script: 0.0 → 0.0% Short, 0.0 → 0.0% Standard (stance changes a match 73 → 130 and 134 → 226). Vs tier IV: 0.3 → 0.5% and 0.3 → 0.3%.
  - `stance_toggler` vs the Balanced script: 20.3 → 32.0% Short, 60.5 → 52.9% Standard (105 → 556 and 178 → 913 changes a match). Vs tier IV: 13.5 → 10.8% and 41.3 → 19.5%.
  - The exploit tool vs tier VII (200 a format): the Balanced control 3.5 / 3.0%, `stance_toggler` 0.0 / 0.0%, `stance_dancer` 0.0 / 0.0%, `fallback_turtle` 0% (both runs).
- **Verdict:** instant switching creates no exploit: dancing faster loses more against the AI, and the only rise (the toggler against the plain script in Short War) stays well under 50% and is a script artefact (it loses harder against real bots). Engagement freshness (4 s without a target) already makes stance resets worthless, so no non-cooldown fix is needed. Gate (A18.12, `tools/exploits.ts` `STANCE_PROXIES`): `stance_toggler` and `stance_dancer` no better than the control +2 points vs tier VII; the report gained a "Stance changes/match" column.

## Owner request 2026-10-07: Army's Available grouped by class (WP9)

- **Rule:** Available shows small headed groups (class icon, name, count pill, hairline): Infantry, Ranged, Heavy, Anti-heavy, Siege, Support, Air, then Turrets, Powers and Forts; empty groups are left out; NEW cards first within a group, then the content order (`availableGroups` in `src/ui/screens/model/armyAge.ts`). Tap, Use, tap-a-slot and drag work as before. The heading names the class, so the cards in a group drop their class line.
- **Layout:** groups sit side by side and wrap like words, so a group of one or two cards never leaves a row half empty, and a big group takes the width and wraps its cards. On phones the headings are 16 px with no gap above, the pool's top padding is 2 px and the body's bottom padding 4 px, so the headings cost no more height than the old hint line: the first row's two-line names stay whole at 844 × 390 (checked at 844 × 390, 844 × 340, 800 × 360 and 1280 × 720 with seven troop slots). When a Legendary is in the pool, every group's first row keeps 12 px for its crown so it never covers a heading and side-by-side rows stay level.
- **Fix found on the way:** the groups had a fade-in animation, which made each group a stacking context, so a selected card's action bar slid under the next group's heading; the groups no longer animate (their cards still rise in).

## Owner feedback 2026-10-07: a real destroyed-base collapse (WP4 visuals, WP5 render, WP6 audio)

- **Owner:** the destroyed base only slid a little into the ground, faded and squashed. It must really collapse, at the quality of a polished cartoon mobile game.
- **Technique (DESIGN A11 Base collapse, B5):** a hybrid. (1) The base's own current frame (team underlay, main frame, skin tint, crumble stage) is cut at runtime into 20-24 seeded, jittered Voronoi cells (shared zig-zag edges, so they tile without gaps) and drawn as Pixi meshes over the frame's UVs, with a dark cut-face rim. (2) A small 2D rigid-piece world in `src/visuals/adapters/world/collapse/world.ts`: a fixed 4 ms step on an integer-microsecond accumulator, gravity, one impulse at the deepest vertex against the ground and a heightfield of the settled pile, friction and rolling damping, sleep, a back wall at the world's end, and topple groups that rotate about a hinge under their centre of mass (it tips and drops, not a lid swinging on a far corner), releasing their pieces from the top as they fall. (3) Per-age Blender collapse kits (`art/blender/world/base_collapse.py`, `render_collapse.py`): 8 debris pieces, 2 rubble heaps and a team-masked banner scrap, about 15 KB per age, the `collapse` clip of `base.<age>`, loaded once that base reaches crumble stage 2. (4) A capped particle field: dust ring, dust column, impact dust, trickle puffs, smoke and smoke lobes, fire and flames, embers, sparks, shield shards and arcs, splinters, Treasury coins, rags.
- **Rejected:** a pre-rendered collapse animation per age (8 ages × 4 crumble stages × skins and decorations × both sides would be megabytes of frames, and it could not show the base's actual skin or decorations); code-drawn rocks only (generic, it does not look like the base that falls); a physics library (bundle size and far more than this needs).
- **Beats and feel (DESIGN A12):** `collapseWith({ seed })` (duck-typed on the atlas base view) returns `{ breakMs, landMs, settleMs, material }`; the break is at 520 ms (600 with Reduce motion), the landing comes from a dry run of the same seeded world. The render layer fires the feel on those beats in game time (frozen by the hit-stop, slowed by slow motion, faster at 2x), so sound, camera and pieces stay together: at the end a light shake (+0.22), 6 debris and `base_doom_rumble`; at the break a 130 ms exempt hit-stop, 0.38x slow motion for 980 ms easing back over its last 700 ms (`SlowMotion` gained `easeMs`), trauma 1, a 90 ms base flash plus a soft warm screen flash (110 ms, 30%), a 7% camera punch over 460 ms (`Camera.punch`), 24 debris, `base_destroyed` with `base_break_<material>` and, 200 ms later, `base_debris_<material>`, the music ducked 8 dB for 1.8 s; the cheer and the victory/defeat stinger 340 ms after the break (they used to play on the end itself, over the crash); `base_settle_thud` and +0.38 on the landing. The Result hand-off is unchanged (the app's 2.5 s); the landing comes at about 1.8 s and the pile is still by about 2.5 s of real time, so nothing needed to wait longer or be skippable.
- **Camera:** the destroyed-base push is 1.3x (was 1.45x) framed on the 320 lu base: about 1.25x on desktop; a phone's band is shorter than the base, so it eases back to as low as 0.86x and keeps the ground line in place. The base stays at the world's right end on screen (the camera never passes the world edge), and the towers fall toward the lane, into the frame.
- **Determinism and replays:** the art's randomness comes from `collapseSeed(match seed, side, end tick)` (xmur3; the end tick from the outcome) and separate seeded streams for anticipation, break, impacts, aftermath and physics; the fixed step makes the same collapse at any frame rate (tests).
- **Reduce motion:** no tremble, blast, shards or sparks; the pieces subside with a fade into the dust; pebbles drop without spinning; no hit-stop, slow motion, shake, flashes, camera push or punch (the camera only pans to the base). **Lite:** 55% of the cells (about 12-17 chunks), no rims, fewer emitters, a 150-particle cap (320 on High).
- **Turrets and dressing:** each turret rides its chunk (`mountPose(i)`) and pops off ('sell' clip) as it lands; the base dressing's flag shivers in the build-up, snaps on the break and topples (`collapseAt(breakMs)`).
- **Audio (DESIGN A13):** `base_destroyed` redesigned as the shared crash, plus 10 new ids in the `match` sheet (`tools/audio/sfx/sounds_collapse.py`, rendered with the audio tools' synthesis and soundfont kit, packed by `build.py`; ZzFX fallbacks with 3 variants in `src/audio/sounds.ts`). Loudness: the crash -16 LUFS (the loudest moment of a match), the material layer -18, the rumble -21, the debris -22/-23, the thud -19. The `match` sheet grows from 598 to 732 KB of Opus (812 to 1,004 KB of AAC; a browser downloads one), 26 s for 11 sounds with 2 variants each; only the `match` sheet and `assets.gen.ts` changed.
- **Frame cost** (dev sandbox, real sim and art, Chromium with SwiftShader at 844 × 390, Industrial on High; in brackets with 4x CPU throttling for a mid phone): the end frame pays 8-10 ms for the fracture and the world (35 ms), the next two frames 11-13 ms for the deferred dry run and the chunk meshes (21-23 ms); after that the collapse costs 0.45 ms a frame on average, 0.9 ms p95 (1.5-2 ms, 3-4 ms). Draw calls go from 21 to 22-23 on average (30 at most); the collapse keeps at most about 250-290 of its 320 particles (Lite about 120 of 150). Found and fixed on the way: chunk meshes over 100 vertices took Pixi's mesh pipeline, whose shader compiled on the frame of the break (seconds in SwiftShader), so they are always batched; the base-flash `ColorMatrixFilter` compiled on its first visible use (the first base hit, or the break), so it is drawn once as an invisible speck in the first frames of a battle; the collapse code was always cold when a base fell, so a small collapse runs once when the first base view is made (the Home diorama or the battle load); dust, most of the collapse's fill, was cut by about a third. Not measured: a real phone GPU (SwiftShader stalls on fill now and then on High, rarely on Lite).
- **Tests:** `src/visuals/test/collapse.test.ts` (fracture determinism and tiling, frame-rate independence, seeds, beats, topple direction and settling for every age, particle caps, the reduce-motion path, the kits in the manifest and on disk, the atlas base view's live beats, mount poses and seeding); `src/render/test/eventMapper.test.ts` (the end, the break, the stinger and the landing, the defeat stinger, every material's sounds), `battleView.test.ts` (seed from match data, beat order and timing, the hit-stop, slow motion and punch, reduce motion, 2x speed), `feel.test.ts` (eased slow motion, the collapse rules and their validation), `cameraPush.test.ts` (the punch, the framing pull-back); the A13 id lists in `sounds.test.ts` (377 ids) and `designIds.ts`.
- **Dev tools:** the battle sandbox gained `w1.<age>` formats, a `baseBp` cheat and frame-exact `manual(on)` / `step(ms)` for the capture scripts (dev only).
- **Kept open:** the code-drawn (procedural) base tier keeps its old sink-and-fade collapse (it only shows when the atlas sheets fail to load); the kits' colour-rule numbers are base-exempt materials (A11).
